) as

(3.73)

\displaystyle\frac{i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}(p)}{2}-\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}(p)}{2(1+\gamma_{t}(p))}\,=

(3.74)

\displaystyle\Big(E(p)+\frac{\lambda}{N}\Gamma^{(2)}_{t}*\big(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0)\big)(p)\Big)\sigma_{t}(p)

(3.75)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\Big(\big(\Sigma^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v}\big)(p)(1+\gamma_{t}(p))\,+\,\big(\overline{\Sigma}^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v}\big)(p)\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)^{2}}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\Big)\,.

We recognize that (
3.75
) is a renormalization of (
3.36
), where
(\Gamma^{(1)},\Sigma^{(1)})
is replaced by the renormalized fields
(\Gamma^{(2)},\Sigma^{(2)})
. In particular, the proof of Lemma
A.4
implies that (
3.75
) is equivalent to

(3.76)

\displaystyle\begin{split}i\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}\,=&\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big[\big(\Sigma^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v}\big)\,\overline{\sigma}_{t}\,-\,\big(\overline{\Sigma}^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v}\big)\,\sigma_{t}\big]\,,\\
i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}\,=&\,2\big(E+\frac{\lambda}{N}\Gamma^{(2)}_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)\sigma_{t}\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\Sigma^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v}\big)(1+2\gamma_{t})\,.\end{split}

Recalling (
3.56
), (
3.57
) and (
3.58
), we thus have shown that

(3.77)

\displaystyle\begin{split}i\partial_{t}\phi^{(2)}_{t}\,=&\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\Big(\big(\Gamma^{(2)}_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(0)\phi^{(2)}_{t}\,+\,(\Sigma^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v})(0)\overline{\phi}^{(2)}_{t}\Big)\\
&-\,2\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(0)|\phi^{(2)}_{t}|^{2}\phi^{(2)}_{t}\,.\\
\partial_{t}\gamma^{(2)}_{t}\,=&\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Im}\big((\Sigma^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v})\overline{\sigma}^{(2)}_{t}\big)\,,\\
i\partial_{t}\sigma^{(2)}_{t}\,=&\,2\big(E+\frac{\lambda}{N}\Gamma^{(2)}_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)\sigma^{(2)}_{t}\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\Sigma^{(2)}_{t}*\hat{v}\big)(1+2\gamma^{(2)}_{t})\,.\end{split}

(
3.77
) corresponds to the second order renormalization of (
3.39
) with the corresponding renormalized fields.

Employing Lemma
A.2
, see also (

), we obtain

(3.80)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Omega^{(2)}_{t}(p)\,=\,\\
&\Big(E(p)+\frac{\lambda}{N}\big((\gamma^{(2)}_{t}+N|\Lambda||\phi^{(2)}_{t}|^{2}\delta)*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(p)\Big)\big(1+2\gamma^{(2)}_{t}(p)\big)\\
&+\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Re}\Big(\big((\overline{\sigma}^{(2)}_{t}+N|\Lambda|(\overline{\phi}^{(2)}_{t})^{2}\delta)*\hat{v}\big)(p)\sigma^{(2)}_{t}(p)\Big)\,-\frac{\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\sigma}^{(2)}_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\sigma^{(2)}_{t}(p)\big)}{1+\gamma^{(2)}_{t}(p)}\\
&+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big((1+2\gamma_{t})f_{0}^{(+)})*\big(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0)\big)(p)(1+2\gamma_{t}(p))\\
&+\,\frac{4\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Re}\Big(\big(f_{0}^{(+)}\overline{\sigma}^{(2)}_{t}\big)*\hat{v}(p)\sigma^{(2)}_{t}(p)\Big)\,.\end{split}

Recalling the total fields (
3.56
), (
3.57
), and employing (
3.77
), we thus conclude that

(3.81)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Omega^{(2)}_{t}\,=\,E+\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\Gamma^{(2)}_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\frac{\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\Sigma}^{(2)}*\hat{v})\sigma^{(2)}_{t}\big)}{1+\gamma^{(2)}_{t}}\,.\end{split}

(
3.81
) corresponds to (
3.40
) with renormalized fields.

3.3.
Boltzmann collision terms

The discussion in section (
3.2
) yields

(3.83)

\displaystyle f_{t}(p)=

\displaystyle f_{0}(p)\,+\,\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{3}[f_{0}](s,p)

(3.84)

\displaystyle\,+\,\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \frac{\langle[[[a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}}{|\Lambda|}\,,

see (
3.49
), denotes the cubic Boltzmann collision operator.

We show in Lemma
C.1
that

(3.86)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{3}[f_{0}](s,p)\,=\\
&2\lambda^{2}\operatorname{Re}\int_{[0,t]^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{2}\ \mathds{1}_{s_{1}\geq s_{2}}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{3}\ \Big(\frac{1}{2!}\big(\delta(p_{1}-p)+\delta(p_{2}-p)-\delta(p_{3}-p)\big)\\
&\mathbf{w}^{(2,1)}_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{p}_{3})\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{(2,1)}_{s_{2}}(\mathbf{p}_{3})e^{i\int_{s_{2}}^{s_{1}}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})\big)}\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})\\
&\big(\widetilde{f_{0}}(p_{1})\widetilde{f_{0}}(p_{2})f_{0}(p_{3})-f_{0}(p_{1})f_{0}(p_{2})\widetilde{f_{0}}(p_{3})\big)\\
&+\,\frac{1}{3!}\big(\delta(p_{1}-p)+\delta(p_{2}-p)+\delta(p_{3}-p)\big)\\
&\mathbf{w}^{(3,0)}_{s_{1}}(\mathbf{p}_{3})\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{(3,0)}_{s_{2}}(\mathbf{p}_{3})e^{i\int_{s_{2}}^{s_{1}}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})\big)}\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})\\
&\big(\widetilde{f_{0}}(p_{1})\widetilde{f_{0}}(p_{2})\widetilde{f_{0}}(p_{3})-f_{0}(p_{1})f_{0}(p_{2})f_{0}(p_{3})\big)\Big)\,.\end{split}

In particular, this provides an explicit expression for the Boltzmann operator in the evolution of
f
.
In Lemma
C.1
, we also give an expression for the quartic Boltzmann term
\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{4}[f_{0}](s,p)
.

3.4.
Error terms

We abbreviate

(3.87)

f[J]\,:=\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ J(p)a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,.

Then we define the error

(3.88)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Rem}[f](t)[J]\,:=

(3.89)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\,-\int\mathrm{d}p\ J(p)\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \Big(\frac{1}{N}Q_{3}[f_{0}](s,p)+\frac{1}{N^{2}}Q_{4}[f_{0}](s,p)\Big)\\
&+\,\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \langle[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s)]\rangle_{0}\\
&+\,\int_{[0,t]^{2}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{2}\ \langle[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})]\rangle_{0}\end{split}

(3.90)

\displaystyle\,-\,\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{4}[f_{0}](s,p)

(3.91)

\displaystyle\,+\,\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \big(Q_{3}[f](s,p)-Q_{3}[f_{0}](s,p)\big)

(3.92)

\displaystyle\,+\,\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \langle[[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}\,.

Using the fact that odd moments of
a^{\#}
w.r.t.
\langle\cdot\rangle_{0}
vanish and rearranging the terms in the first three lines of (
3.89
), yields

(3.93)

&\quad-\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ J(p)\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{4}[f_{0}](s,p)\,.\end{split}

Due to our choice of HFB fields
(\phi^{(2)},\gamma^{(2)},\sigma^{(2)})
satisfying (
3.77
) and the Bogoliubov dispersion
\Omega^{(2)}
satisfying (
3.81
), we have that (
3.93
) vanishes.

(1)

In order to estimate the terms coming from the tail (
3.92
), we compute
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}
,
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(cor)}}
, and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(d)}}
for the HFB fields
(\phi^{(2)},\gamma^{(2)},\sigma^{(2)},\Omega^{(2)})
in Lemma
E.3
. Then we use the following ideas:

(a)

\int\mathrm{d}p\ a_{p}^{\#}\lesssim\mathcal{N}_{b}^{\frac{1}{2}}
and that
a_{p}^{\#}\lesssim(|\Lambda|\mathcal{N}_{b})^{\frac{1}{2}}
, see Lemmata
D.2
,
D.3
, and
D.4
.

(b)

Proposition
5.1
, followed by Lemma
D.1

implies

(3.94)

\displaystyle\langle(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\rangle_{t}\,

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X},\ell}\,e^{K_{\ell,\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}|\Lambda|\lambda t}

(3.95)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\langle(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\Big(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N|\Lambda|}\Big)\rangle_{0}

(3.96)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X},|\Lambda|,\ell}\,e^{K_{\ell,\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}|\Lambda|\lambda t}|\Lambda|^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\,.

With these steps, we obtain

(3.97)

\displaystyle|\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \langle[[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}\Big)|

(3.98)

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X},\|\hat{v}\|_{Y},|\Lambda|}e^{K_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}|\Lambda|\lambda t}\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|J\|_{\infty}

(2)

Due to the dependence of
\frac{1}{N^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{4}[f_{0}](s,p)
on the HFB fields
(\phi_{t},\gamma_{t},\sigma_{t})
, we will employ a priori estimates established in Corollary
4.6
. With that, we can control (
3.90
) by

(3.99)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N^{2}}|\int\mathrm{d}p\ J(p)\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ Q_{4}[f_{0}](s,p)|\,\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}}\,\frac{\lambda^{2}t^{2}}{N^{2}}e^{C_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}t}\|J\|_{\infty}\,.

(3)

In order to estimate (
3.91
), we write
Q_{3}[h]=:Q_{3}[h,h,h]
, in order to emphasize the dependence on three arguments, each evaluated at different momenta. Then we have that

(3.100)

\displaystyle Q_{3}[f,f,f]-Q_{3}[f_{0},f_{0},f_{0}]\,=

\displaystyle\,(Q_{3}[f,f,f]-Q_{3}[f_{0},f,f])

(3.101)

\displaystyle\,+\,(Q_{3}[f_{0},f,f]\,-\,Q_{3}[f_{0},f_{0},f])

(3.102)

\displaystyle\,+\,(Q_{3}[f_{0},f_{0},f]\,-\,Q_{3}[f_{0},f_{0},f_{0}])\,.

Each of the differences contains a factor
f-f_{0}\propto\frac{1}{N}
. Arguing as in
[
37
, Chapter 5.3]
, we then obtain

(3.103)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \big(Q_{3}[f](s)[J]-Q_{3}[f_{0}](s)[J]\big)\Big|\,\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X},\|\hat{v}\|_{Y},|\Lambda|}e^{K_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}|\Lambda|\lambda t}\frac{\|J\|_{\infty}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,.

With these estimates, we obtain

(3.104)

\displaystyle|\operatorname{Rem}[f](t)[J]|\,\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X},\|\hat{v}\|_{Y},|\Lambda|}e^{K_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}|\Lambda|\lambda t}\frac{\|J\|_{\infty}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\,.

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 4.84 L 0 26.29 L 0 26.29 L 0 4.84}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hskip-15.29172pt{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}\vbox to19.00002pt{}{}(s_{2})]\rangle_{0}

We need to collect all terms involving a factor
N^{-\frac{3}{2}}
. Consequently, we need to consider the third-order Duhamel expansion of
\Phi
.

(1)

Due to
[a_{0},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(s_{1})]
being a scalar, we have that

(3.111)

[[[a_{0},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\,=\,0\,.

(2)

(3.112)

\displaystyle\langle[[[a_{0},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{0}\,=

(3.113)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{N}\langle[[a_{2,1}(s_{1})a_{0}+a_{2,2}a^{\dagger}_{0}],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{0}\,\propto\,\frac{\lambda^{3}}{N^{2}}\,,

for
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)\sim\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{N}}
, see Proposition
5.2
.

(3)

Similarly to the previous step, we have that

(3.114)

\displaystyle\langle[[[a_{0},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{0}\,\propto\,\frac{\lambda^{3}}{N^{2}}\,.

(4)

We are left with computing

(3.115)

\displaystyle\langle[[[a_{0},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{0}\,=

(3.116)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{N}}\langle[[f[a_{3,1}(s_{1})]+g[a_{3,2}(s_{1})]+g^{\dagger}[a_{3,3}(s_{1})]],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{0}\,,

where we abbreviated

(3.117)

\displaystyle g[J]\,:=\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ J(p)a_{p}a_{-p}\,,\quad g^{\dagger}[J]\,:=\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ J(p)a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}\,.

Employing Lemma
A.2
, we find that

(3.118)

\displaystyle a_{3,1}(t,p)\,

\displaystyle=\,e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \Omega_{\tau}(0)}\mathbf{w}^{(2,1)}_{t}(0,p,p)\,,

(3.119)

\displaystyle a_{3,2}(t,p)\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{2}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \Omega_{\tau}(0)}\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{(2,1)}_{t}(p,p,0)\,,

(3.120)

\displaystyle a_{3,3}(t,p)\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{2}e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \Omega_{\tau}(0)}\mathbf{w}^{(3,0)}_{t}(p,p,0)\,.

We recognize the evolutions of
f
and
g
, resulting in

(3.121)

\displaystyle\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \mathds{1}_{s_{1}\geq s_{2}\geq s_{3}}\langle[[[a_{0},{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{0}\,=

(3.122)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ e^{i\int_{0}^{s}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \Omega_{\tau}(0)}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big(\mathbf{w}^{(2,1)}_{s}(0,p,p)Q_{3}[f_{0}](s,p)

(3.123)

\displaystyle\quad+\,\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{(2,1)}_{s}(p,p,0)Q_{3}^{(g)}[f_{0}](s,p)\,+\,\mathbf{w}^{(3,0)}_{s}(p,p,0)\overline{Q_{3}^{(g)}[f_{0}](s,p)}\Big)\,.

With analogous steps as above and recalling (
2.97
), we then obtain that

(3.124)

\displaystyle\Phi_{t}\,

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \big(Q_{3}^{(\Phi)}[f](s)+Q_{3,3}^{(\Phi)}[f](s)\big)\,+\,\operatorname{Rem}[\Phi](t)\,,

where

(3.125)

\displaystyle|\operatorname{Rem}[\Phi](t)|\,\lesssim_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X},\|\hat{v}\|_{Y},|\Lambda|}e^{K_{\|f_{0}\|_{X},\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}|\Lambda|\lambda t}\frac{1}{N^{2}}\,.

4.
Estimates on the HFB evolution

In order to proceed as in
[
37
]
, we need to determine a priori bounds for
u
,
v
, and
\phi
. Observe that it suffices to establish bounds for the HFB fields
\phi
and
\gamma
, for
|\sigma|^{2}=(1+\gamma)\gamma
.

We start by rewriting the renormalized HFB equations (
3.39
) and (
3.77
). In both cases, we get a closed system for the total fields
\phi^{(j)}
and

(4.1)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma^{(j)}\\
\Gamma^{(j)}\end{pmatrix}\,=\,\big((1+\delta_{j,2}f_{0}^{(+)})\begin{pmatrix}\sigma^{(j)}\\
\gamma^{(j)}\end{pmatrix}\,+\,\delta_{j,2}f_{0}^{(+)}\,\begin{pmatrix}0\\
1\end{pmatrix}\,+\,N|\Lambda|\delta\begin{pmatrix}(\phi^{(j)})^{2}\\
|\phi_{t}^{(j)}|^{2}\end{pmatrix}\,,

without having to explicitly refer to the reduced fields
(\phi^{(j)},\sigma^{(j)},\gamma^{(j)})
.
Then the HFB equations (
3.39
) and (
3.77
) can be rewritten as

(4.2)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}\,&=\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\Big(\big(\Gamma_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(0)\phi_{t}+(\Sigma_{t}*\hat{v})(0)\overline{\phi}_{t}\Big)-2\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(0)|\phi_{t}|^{2}\phi_{t}\,,\\
\partial_{t}\Gamma_{t}\,&=\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Im}\big((\Sigma_{t}*\hat{v})\overline{\Sigma}_{t}\big)\,,\\
i\partial_{t}\Sigma_{t}\,&=\,2\big(E+\frac{\lambda}{N}\Gamma_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)\Sigma_{t}\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\Sigma_{t}*\hat{v}\big)(1+2\Gamma_{t})\\
&\quad\quad-\,4N\lambda|\Lambda|^{2}\hat{v}(0)|\phi_{t}|^{2}\phi_{t}^{2}\delta\,.\end{cases}

4.1.
Well-posedness theory for the HFB system

Lemma 4.1

(Local well-posedness)
.

Let
(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\in{\mathcal{X}}^{1}
. Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
. Then there exists a maximal existence time
0<T\leq\infty
and a unique maximal mild solution
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)
of (
4.2
) on
[0,T)
. If
T<\infty
, then
\lim_{t\to T-}\|(\phi_{t},\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}=\infty
. The solution depends continuously on the initial datum.

Proof.

We start by abbreviating the nonlinearity

(4.3)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{1}(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma):=

\displaystyle-\,i\Big[\frac{\lambda}{N}\Big(\big(\Gamma*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(0)\phi\,+\,(\Sigma*\hat{v})(0)\overline{\phi}\Big)\,,

(4.4)

\displaystyle-\,2\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(0)|\phi|^{2}\phi\Big]

(4.5)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{2}(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma):=

\displaystyle-\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Im}\Big(\big(\overline{\Sigma}*\hat{v}\big)\,\Sigma\Big)\,,

(4.6)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{J}}_{3}(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma):=

\displaystyle-\,i\Big[2\big(\frac{\lambda}{N}\Gamma*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)\Sigma\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\Sigma*\hat{v}\big)(1+2\Gamma)

(4.7)

\displaystyle-\,4N\lambda|\Lambda|^{2}\hat{v}(0)|\phi|^{2}\phi^{2}\,\delta\Big]\,.

Let
\vec{{\mathcal{J}}}:=({\mathcal{J}}_{1},{\mathcal{J}}_{2},{\mathcal{J}}_{3})
, and
\diag(a,b,c)
denote the diagonal
3\times 3
matrix with entries
a
,
b
,
c
, and define

(4.8)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{\Gamma},\widetilde{\Sigma})\,:=\,

(4.9)

\displaystyle(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},e^{-2iEt}\Sigma_{0})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \vec{{\mathcal{J}}}(\widetilde{\phi}_{s},\widetilde{\Gamma}_{s},\widetilde{\Sigma}_{s})\diag(1,1,e^{-2iE(t-s)})\,.

A standard calculation, explained in Lemma
B.2
, yields

(4.10)

\displaystyle\|\vec{{\mathcal{J}}}(\phi_{1},\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1})\,-\,\vec{{\mathcal{J}}}(\phi_{2},\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}

(4.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\Big(\frac{\lambda}{N}(\|\hat{v}\|_{L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}}+\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty})\big(\|(\phi_{1},\Gamma_{1},\Sigma_{1})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}+\|(\phi_{2},\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{2})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}\big)

(4.12)

\displaystyle\|(\phi_{1}-\phi_{2},\Gamma_{1}-\Gamma_{2},\Sigma_{1}-\Sigma_{2})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}\,+\,\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(0)(|\phi_{1}|^{2}+|\phi_{2}|^{2})

(4.13)

\displaystyle\big(1+N|\Lambda|^{\frac{3}{2}}(|\phi_{1}|+|\phi_{2}|)\big)|\phi_{1}-\phi_{2}|\,.

With the previous estimates, we can apply the standard contraction mapping arguments to
{\mathcal{L}}
to show the existence of a unique solution
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)\cap C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\big)
as long as
T>0
is small enough. In addition, in the usual way, one can show a blow-up alternative, i.e., if the maximal time
T>0
of existence is finite, we have that
\lim_{t\to T-}\|(\phi_{t},\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}=\infty
. With standard arguments, we also show the continuous dependence on initial data.
∎

In order to obtain an expression for the HFB energy functional, we look at the phase factor

(4.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}S_{t}\,=\,

(4.15)

\displaystyle|\Lambda|\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[E(p)\gamma_{t}(p)\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\Gamma^{(1)}_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))(p)\Gamma^{(1)}_{t}(p)

(4.16)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\overline{\Sigma}^{(1)}_{t}*\hat{v}(p)\Sigma^{(1)}_{t}(p)\,-\,N|\Lambda|^{2}\lambda|\phi_{t}|^{4}\hat{v}(p)\delta(p)

(4.17)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{N|\Lambda|\delta(p)\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\phi}_{t}i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}\big)}{2}\,-\,\frac{\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}(p)\big)}{2\big(1+\gamma_{t}(p)\big)}\Big]\,.

obtained in Lemma
A.2
. Observe that the terms not involving any time derivatives correspond to the sum over all complete contractions of
{\mathcal{H}}_{N}
with expectations

(4.18)

\displaystyle\langle a_{p}\rangle=

\displaystyle\phi\delta(p)\,,

(4.19)

\displaystyle\langle a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\rangle=

\displaystyle|\Lambda|\Gamma^{(1)}(p)\,,

(4.20)

\displaystyle\langle a_{p}a_{-p}\rangle=

\displaystyle|\Lambda|\Sigma^{(1)}(p)\,,

see, e.g.,
[
8
]
. Moreover, we can replace
E(p)\gamma_{t}(p)
in (
4.17
) by
E(p)\Gamma^{(1)}_{t}(p)
. In particular, (
4.17
) gives rise to the
HFB energy (density) functional

(4.21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma):=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big(E(p)\Gamma(p)\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\Gamma*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))(p)\Gamma(p)

(4.22)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\overline{\Sigma}*\hat{v}(p)\Sigma(p)\,-\,N|\Lambda|^{2}\lambda|\phi|^{4}\hat{v}(p)\delta(p)\Big)\,.

In addition, we define the
(total) HFB density

(4.23)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{1}\,:=\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Gamma(p)\,.

Lemma 4.2

(Conservation laws)
.

Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
. Let
(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\in{\mathcal{X}}^{1}
, and
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)\cap C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\big)
denote the maximal mild solution of (
4.2
). Then the HFB density
\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{1}
and HFB energy
{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{t},\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})
are differentiable in time and conserved.

Since the arguments needed for this result are standard, we omit the details at this point. For the interested reader, we elaborate on these details after the proof of Lemma
B.2
.

In order to close this section on the well-posedness of the HFB equations, recall that

(4.24)

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}\,=\,|v_{t}|^{2}\geq 0\,,\quad|\sigma_{t}|^{2}\,=\,u_{t}^{2}|v_{t}|^{2}\,=\,(1+\gamma_{t})\gamma_{t}\,.

Then we have that

(4.25)

\displaystyle\Gamma\,\geq\,\gamma\,\geq\,0\,,

and

(4.26)

\displaystyle|\Sigma|^{2}=

\displaystyle|\sigma+N|\Lambda|\phi^{2}\delta|^{2}

(4.27)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\big(|\sigma|^{2}+(N|\Lambda||\phi|^{2}\delta)^{2}\big)

(4.28)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\big((\gamma+1)\gamma+(N|\Lambda||\phi|^{2}\delta)^{2}\big)

(4.29)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2(\Gamma+1)\Gamma\,,

where we applied Cauchy-Schwarz in the second line. As a consequence, Young’s inequality implies

(4.30)

\displaystyle|\Sigma|\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2(\Gamma+1)\Gamma}

(4.31)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}(\Gamma+1)\,.

For the next statement, we recall from (
2.57
) the truncated fields

(4.32)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{T}\,=\,\Gamma-N|\Lambda||\phi|^{2}\delta\,,\quad\Sigma^{T}\,=\,\Sigma-N|\Lambda|\phi^{2}\delta\,.

Lemma 4.3

(Conditional global well-posedness)
.

Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
, and that
v\geq 0
. Let
(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\in{\mathcal{X}}^{1}
. Let
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)
be the associated unique maximal mild solution of (
4.2
) with existence time
T_{0}>0
, and assume that the truncated expectations satisfy
\Gamma^{T}\geq 0
,
|\Sigma^{T}|^{2}\leq(\Gamma^{T}+1)\Gamma^{T}
. Then
T_{0}=\infty
.

Proof.

Mass-conservation and
\Gamma^{T}\geq 0
imply that

(4.33)

\displaystyle N|\Lambda||\phi_{t}|^{2}\,\leq

\displaystyle\,\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{1}

(4.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}\,,

which is why

(4.35)

\displaystyle|\phi_{t}|\,\leq\,C_{\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}}\,.

Next,

(4.36)

\displaystyle\langle\Sigma_{s}*\hat{v},\Sigma_{s}\rangle\,=\,\int dx\,|\widecheck{\Sigma}_{s}(x)|^{2}v(x)\,,

see (
B.81
), and positivity of
v
yield

(4.37)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{t},\Sigma_{t},\Gamma_{t})\geq

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big(E(p)\Gamma_{t}(p)\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\Gamma^{T}_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))(p)\Gamma^{T}_{t}(p)

(4.38)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\overline{\Sigma}_{t}*\hat{v}(p)\Sigma_{t}(p)

(4.39)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ E(p)\Gamma_{t}(p)\,.

Because of energy and mass conservation, we thus have that

(4.40)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{L^{1}_{1+E}}\,\leq\,C_{\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})}\,.

Using
\Gamma\geq 0
and (
4.31
), we find that

(4.41)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma_{t}(p)\,\leq\,\frac{4\lambda}{N}\|(\Gamma_{t}(p)+1)*\hat{v}\|_{\infty}(\Gamma_{t}(p)+1)\,.

In particular, mass conservation implies

(4.42)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma_{t}(p)\,\leq\,\frac{4\lambda}{N}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{v}\|_{1})(\Gamma_{t}(p)+1)

Gronwall’s inequality then yields the point-wise bound

(4.43)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{t}(p)\,\leq\,e^{\frac{4\lambda}{N}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{v}\|_{1})t}(\Gamma_{t}(p)+1)\,,

which, in turn, gives the uniform bound

(4.44)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{\infty}\,\leq\,e^{\frac{4\lambda}{N}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{v}\|_{1})t}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{\infty}+1)\,.

Finally, employing (
2.32
), we find that

(4.45)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{1+E}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle 2\int\mathrm{d}p\ (\Gamma_{t}(p)+1)\Gamma_{t}(p)(1+E(p))\,.

Collecting (
4.40
) and (
4.44
), we thus obtain

(4.46)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{1+E}}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C_{\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1},{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0}),\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{\infty}}e^{\frac{4\lambda}{N}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{v}\|_{1})t}\,.

(
4.31
) and (
4.44
) imply

(4.47)

\displaystyle\|\Sigma_{t}\|_{\infty}\,\leq\,2e^{\frac{4\lambda}{N}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{v}\|_{1})t}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{\infty}+1)\,.

In particular, we have that
\|(\phi_{t},\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})\|_{{\mathcal{X}}^{1}}<\infty
for all
t\geq 0
, which, by the blow-up alternative in Lemma
4.1
, implies
T_{0}=\infty
.
∎

4.2.
Symplectic description of HFB equations

In this section, we follow closely the ideas in
[
8
]
.
We invoke a symplectic description as a convenient path to establishing global well-posedness.
Let

(4.48)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{t}\,:=\,\begin{pmatrix}\Gamma^{T}_{t}&\Sigma^{T}_{t}\\
\overline{\Sigma}^{T}_{t}&1+\Gamma^{T}_{t}\end{pmatrix}\,,

where we recall the definitions (
2.57
) of
\Gamma^{T}
and
\Sigma^{T}
. Observe that we have that

(4.49)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}\,\geq\,0\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\Gamma^{T}\geq 0\,\wedge|\Sigma^{T}|^{2}\,\leq\,(\Gamma^{T}+1)\Gamma^{T}\,.

The goal of this section is to prove that
{\mathcal{R}}_{0}\geq 0
implies
{\mathcal{R}}_{t}\geq 0
along the flow induced by the HFB equations (
4.2
). More precisely, let

(4.50)

\displaystyle h_{\Gamma}:=

\displaystyle E\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\Gamma*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\,,

(4.51)

\displaystyle h_{\Sigma}:=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{N}\Sigma*\hat{v}\,,

(4.52)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma,\Sigma)}:=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}h_{\Gamma}&h_{\Sigma}\\
\overline{h_{\Sigma}}&h_{\Gamma}\end{pmatrix}\,,

(4.53)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{S}}:=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}\,.

A straightforward calculation yields

(4.54)

\displaystyle i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}={\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}-{\mathcal{R}}_{t}{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{S}}\,.

Let

(4.55)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}&={\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}\,,\\
{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{0}&={\bf 1}\,.\end{cases}

We introduce the function space

(4.56)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{Y}}^{j}\,:=\,\Big\{\begin{pmatrix}a&b\\
\overline{b}&a\end{pmatrix}\mid a\in L^{1}_{(1+E)^{j}}({\Lambda^{*}})\,,\,b\in L^{2}_{(1+E)^{j}}({\Lambda^{*}})\Big\}\,.

Lemma 4.4

(Well-posedness of (
4.55
))
.

Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
. Let
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)
. Then there is a unique solution
{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}\in C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)
of (
4.55
).

Proof.

We have that
E{\mathcal{S}}
is the generator of a continuous semi-group on
{\mathcal{Y}}^{1}
. In addition,
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)
and the estimates in the proof of Lemma
B.2
imply the continuity of
t\mapsto{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}-E{\mathcal{S}}\in{\mathcal{Y}}^{1}
. Finally, we apply standard functional results, e.g., as found in
[
65
]
, in order to show the well-posedness and regularity of
{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}
.
∎

Now let
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)\cap C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\big)
be the solution of (
4.2
). Using Lemma
4.4
, we have that

(4.57)

\displaystyle i\partial_{t}({\mathcal{V}}_{t}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t})\,=

\displaystyle\,-{\mathcal{V}}_{t}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}\,+\,{\mathcal{V}}_{t}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}

(4.58)

\displaystyle+\,{\mathcal{V}}_{t}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}\,-\,{\mathcal{V}}_{t}{\mathcal{R}}_{t}{\mathcal{H}}^{(\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})}{\mathcal{S}}{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}

(4.59)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,0\,.

In particular, provided
{\mathcal{R}}_{0}\geq 0
, we have that

(4.60)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}_{t}\,=\,{\mathcal{V}}^{\dagger}_{t}{\mathcal{R}}_{0}{\mathcal{V}}_{t}\,\geq\,0\,.

Together with (
4.49
), we have thus proved Proposition
2.7
.

4.3.
Bounds on the HFB fields

After establishing global well-posedness of (
4.2
), as well as energy and mass conservation, we are ready to establish bounds on
\Gamma^{T}
and
\phi
. As described at the beginning of section, these, in turn, will yield a bound on
\gamma
, and thus on
u
and
v
. For that, we establish the next result. Recall from (
2.57
) that

\Gamma^{T}=\Gamma-N|\Lambda||\phi|^{2}\delta
,
\Sigma^{T}=\Sigma-N|\Lambda|\phi^{2}\delta
.

Lemma 4.5

(Mass transfer bound)
.

Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
, and that
v\geq 0
. Let
(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\in{\mathcal{X}}^{1}
with
\Gamma^{T}_{0}\geq 0
,
|\Sigma^{T}_{0}|^{2}\leq\sqrt{(\Gamma^{T}_{0}+1)\Gamma^{T}_{0}}
, and
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)\cap C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\big)
denote the global solution of (
4.2
). Then we have that

(4.61)

\displaystyle|\phi_{t}|^{2}\,=

\displaystyle\,|\phi_{0}|^{2}\,+\,\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}-\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}}{N|\Lambda|}\,,

(4.62)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{T}_{t}\,\leq

\displaystyle\,e^{2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+2)t}(\Gamma^{T}_{0}+1)\,,

(4.63)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}\,\leq

\displaystyle\,e^{2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+1)t}(\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}+1)\,,

(4.64)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ E(p)\Gamma_{t}(p)\,\leq

\displaystyle\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\,,

and
|\Sigma^{T}_{t}|\,\leq\,\sqrt{(\Gamma^{T}_{t}+1)\Gamma^{T}_{t}}
.

Proof.

Observe that we have
\|\Gamma\|_{1}=\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Gamma(p)
for
\Gamma\geq 0
. Mass conservation implies

(4.65)

\displaystyle|\phi_{t}|^{2}\,=\,\frac{\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}-\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}}{N|\Lambda|}\,=\,|\phi_{0}|^{2}\,+\,\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}-\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}}{N|\Lambda|}\,.

Notice that Lemma
2.7
yields

(4.66)

\displaystyle|\Sigma^{T}_{t}|\,\leq\,\sqrt{(\Gamma^{T}_{t}+1)\Gamma^{T}_{t}}\,\leq\,\Gamma^{T}_{t}+1,

due to Young’s inequality. (
4.2
) implies

(4.67)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma^{T}_{t}\,=\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Im}\big((\Sigma_{t}*\hat{v})\Sigma^{T}_{t}\big)\,.

Using (
4.66
), Young’s inequality yields

(4.68)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma^{T}_{t}\,\leq

\displaystyle\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}(\|\hat{v}\|_{1}+\|\hat{v}\|_{\infty})(\|\Gamma_{t}\|_{1}+1)(\Gamma^{T}_{t}+1)

(4.69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}+1)(\Gamma^{T}_{t}+1)\,,

where we employed mass conservation. We thus obtain, via Gronwall’s inequality and using the fact
\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}=\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}+N
, that

(4.70)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{T}_{t}\,\leq\,e^{2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\big(\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+2\big)t}(\Gamma^{T}_{0}+1)\,.

Similarly, (
4.67
), together with (
4.66
) and (
4.65
), yields

(4.71)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}\,=

\displaystyle\,2\lambda|\Lambda|\operatorname{Im}\big(\int\mathrm{d}p\ (\Sigma_{t}*\hat{v})(p)\overline{\Sigma}^{T}_{t}(p)\big)

(4.72)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,2\lambda|\Lambda|\operatorname{Im}\big((\overline{\Sigma}^{T}_{t}*\hat{v})(0)\phi_{t}^{2}\big)

(4.73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\frac{\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{1}-\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}}{N}(\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}+1)\,,

where, analogously to (
B.81
), we used that
\int\mathrm{d}p\ (\Sigma^{T}_{t}*\hat{v})(p)\overline{\Sigma}^{T}_{t}(p)\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Then Gronwall’s inequality implies

(4.74)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{T}_{t}\|_{1}\,\leq\,e^{2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+1)t}(\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}+1)\,.

(
4.39
) together with energy conservation implies

(4.75)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ E(p)\Gamma_{t}(p)\,\leq\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{t},\Gamma_{t},\Sigma_{t})\,=\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\,.

This concludes the proof.
∎

As a direct consequence, we obtain the following result.

Corollary 4.6

(Bounds on Bogoliubov coefficients)
.

Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
. Let
(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\in{\mathcal{X}}^{1}
with
\Gamma^{T}_{0}\geq 0
,
|\Sigma^{T}_{0}|^{2}\leq\sqrt{(\Gamma^{T}_{0}+1)\Gamma^{T}_{0}}
, and
(\phi,\Gamma,\Sigma)\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)\cap C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\big)
denote the global solution of (
4.2
). Let
\gamma_{t},\sigma_{t}:{\Lambda^{*}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
with
0\leq\gamma_{t}\leq\Gamma^{T}_{t}
and
|\sigma_{t}|^{2}=(\gamma_{t}+1)\gamma_{t}
be given, and define

(4.76)

\displaystyle u_{t}(p)\,

\displaystyle:=\,\sqrt{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\,,

(4.77)

\displaystyle v_{t}(p)\,

\displaystyle:=\,\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)}{\sqrt{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}}\,,

i.e.,
|v_{t}|^{2}=\gamma_{t}
. Then we have that

(4.78)

\displaystyle 1\,+\,\|\gamma_{t}\|_{\infty}\,=\,1+\|v_{t}\|_{\infty}^{2}\,=\,\|u_{t}\|_{\infty}^{2}\,

\displaystyle\leq\,1\,+\,e^{2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+2)t}(\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{\infty}+1)\,,

(4.79)

\displaystyle\|v_{t}\|_{2}^{2}\,=\,\|\gamma_{t}\|_{1}\,

\displaystyle\leq\,e^{2\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+1)t}\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}\,,

(4.80)

\displaystyle\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{E}}^{2}\,=\,\|\gamma_{t}\|_{L^{1}_{E}}\,

\displaystyle\leq\,{\mathcal{E}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(\phi_{0},\Gamma_{0},\Sigma_{0})\,.

5.
Tail estimates

In this section, we bound the tail in the perturbation expansion
, i.e., (
3.18
) and the corresponding terms in the evolutions of
\Phi
and
g
.
Recall from (
3.56
) and (
3.57
) that

(5.1)

\displaystyle\Gamma^{(2)}\,

\displaystyle=\,(1+2f_{0}^{(+)})\gamma^{(2)}+f_{0}^{(+)}+N|\Lambda||\phi^{(2)}|^{2}\delta\,,

(5.2)

\displaystyle\Sigma^{(2)}\,

\displaystyle=\,(1+2f_{0}^{(+)})\sigma^{(2)}+N|\Lambda|(\phi^{(2)})^{2}\delta\,.

Proposition 5.1

(Bound on Fluctuation dynamics)
.

Assume that
\hat{v}\in L^{1}_{\sqrt{1+E}}\cap L^{\infty}({\Lambda^{*}})
, and that
|\Lambda|\geq 1
. Let
(\phi^{(2)},\Gamma^{(2)},\Sigma^{(2)})\in C^{0}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\big)\cap C^{1}_{t}\big([0,T),{\mathcal{X}}^{-1}\big)
denote the global solution of (
4.2
) with initial datum

(5.3)

(\phi^{(2)}_{0},\Gamma^{(2)}_{0},\Sigma^{(2)}_{0})\,=\,(\phi_{0},(1+2f_{0}^{(+)})\gamma_{0}+f_{0}^{(+)}+N|\Lambda||\phi_{0}|^{2}\delta,(1+2f_{0}^{(+)})\sigma_{0}+N|\Lambda|\phi_{0}^{2}\delta)\in{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\,.

Then for any
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
, there exist constants
C_{\ell},K_{\ell}>0
such that for all
t>0
, we have that

(5.4)

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\big(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N|\Lambda|}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|

(5.5)

\displaystyle\leq\,C_{\ell}\Big(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{2}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{2}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{\ell}

(5.6)

\displaystyle\qquad e^{K_{\ell}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\lambda|\Lambda|(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\,.

Proof.

Lemma
E.1
implies

(5.7)

\displaystyle\big\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}{\mathcal{T}}[k_{0}](\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\big\|\,\lesssim_{\ell}

\displaystyle\,(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{1}+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{\infty})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\,,

(5.8)

\displaystyle\big\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}](\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\big\|\,\lesssim_{\ell}

\displaystyle\,(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{1}+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{\infty})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\,.

By definition (
2.57
), we have that
(\Gamma^{(2)})^{T}=(1+2f_{0}^{(+)})\gamma^{(2)}+f_{0}^{(+)}
. Corollary
4.6
then implies

(5.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}](\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\big\|\\
&\lesssim_{\ell}\,e^{\ell\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{\|(\Gamma^{(2)}_{0})^{T}\|_{1}}{N}+1)t}(1+\|(\Gamma^{(2)}_{0})^{T}\|_{1}+\|(\Gamma^{(2)}_{0})^{T}\|_{\infty})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\\
&\lesssim_{\ell}\,e^{\ell\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(\frac{(1+2\|f_{0}\|_{\infty})\|\gamma_{0}\|_{1}+\|f_{0}\|_{1}}{N}+1)t}(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\,.\end{split}

Lemma
E.2
implies that for
\widehat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{N}(t):={\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]e^{-it{\mathcal{H}}_{N}}{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{0}]
, we have that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}\widehat{{\mathcal{U}}}_{N}(t)(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\big(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N|\Lambda|}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|\\
&\leq\,C_{\ell}\Big(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{2}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{2}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}e^{K_{\ell}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\lambda|\Lambda|(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\,.\end{split}

Collecting (
5.7
), (
5.9
), and (
5.10
), we obtain

(5.11)

\displaystyle\Big\|(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{-\frac{\ell}{2}}\big(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N|\Lambda|}\big)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\Big\|

(5.12)

\displaystyle\leq\,C_{\ell}\Big(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{2}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{2}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{\frac{\ell}{2}}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{\ell}

(5.13)

\displaystyle\qquad e^{K_{\ell}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\lambda|\Lambda|(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\,,

which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.2
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
5.1
, we have that

(5.14)

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(t)P_{n}\|}{\sqrt{n+|\Lambda|}},\,\sqrt{N}\frac{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(cor)}}(t)P_{n}\|}{n+|\Lambda|},\,\sqrt{N}\frac{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(d)}}(t)P_{n}\|}{n},\,\frac{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}(t)P_{n}\|}{(n+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{3}{2}}},\,\sqrt{N}\frac{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(t)P_{n}\|}{(n+|\Lambda|)^{2}}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\leq\,C\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{N}}e^{C\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\|f_{0}\|_{X}(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{2}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{2}\,.

Proof.

Recalling from (
2.32
) that
|\sigma|^{2}=(1+\gamma)\gamma
, Lemmata
E.3
and
D.4
imply

(5.16)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(t)P_{n}\|\,\leq

\displaystyle\,C\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{\sqrt{N}}(u_{t}(0)+|v_{t}(0)|)|\phi_{t}|

(5.17)

\displaystyle\,(|(f_{0}^{(+)}\sigma_{t})*\hat{v}(0)|+|\big((1+2\gamma_{t})f_{0}^{(+)}\big)*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))(0)|)\sqrt{n+1}

(5.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{\sqrt{N}}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(u_{t}(0)+|v_{t}(0)|)|\phi_{t}|\|f_{0}\|_{X}(1+\|\gamma_{t}\|_{X})\sqrt{n+1}\,.

Now we employ Lemma
4.5
and Corollary
4.6
, and obtain

(5.19)

\displaystyle\frac{\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(t)P_{n}\|}{\sqrt{n+1}}\,\leq

\displaystyle\,C\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{N}}e^{C\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N})t}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\|f_{0}\|_{X}\Big(1+\frac{\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{1}}{N}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+\|\Gamma^{T}_{0}\|_{X})^{\frac{3}{2}}

(5.20)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{N}}e^{C\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}\|f_{0}\|_{X}(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{2}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{2}\ .

The bounds on
\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(cor)}}(t)P_{n}\|
and
\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(d)}}(t)P_{n}\|
, see Lemma
E.3
, and on
\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}(t)P_{n}\|
and
\|{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(t)P_{n}\|
, see Lemma
A.2
, can be obtained in an analogous fashion, using Lemmata
D.3
and
D.4
, respectively. This concludes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.3
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
5.1
, and for
|\Lambda|\leq N
, we have that

(5.21)

\displaystyle|\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \langle[[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}|

(5.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C_{\|f_{0}\|_{X}}\|J\|_{2}\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{C\lambda|\Lambda|\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{6}|\Lambda|^{\frac{11}{2}}\,.

Proof.

Proposition
5.2
and Lemma
D.2
imply

(5.23)

\displaystyle|\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \langle[[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}|

(5.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\|J\|_{2}\frac{\lambda^{3}t^{3}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{C\lambda\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}^{3}\|f_{0}\|_{X}^{3}

(5.25)

\displaystyle\,(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{6}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{6}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\langle(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{1+\frac{9}{2}}\Big(1+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{3}\rangle_{s}\,.

Proposition
5.1
then yields

(5.26)

\displaystyle|\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \langle[[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}|

(5.27)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C\|J\|_{2}\frac{\lambda^{3}t^{3}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{C\lambda|\Lambda|\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}^{3}\|f_{0}\|_{X}^{3}

(5.28)

\displaystyle\,(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})^{6}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{6}\langle(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{11}{2}}\Big(1+\frac{(\mathcal{N}_{b}+|\Lambda|)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\sqrt{N}}\Big)^{3}\Big(1+\frac{\mathcal{N}_{b}}{N|\Lambda|}\Big)\rangle_{0}\,.

Finally, we employ Lemma
D.1
and obtain

(5.29)

\displaystyle|\int_{[0,t]^{3}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{s}_{3}\ \langle[[[f[J],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{1})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{2})],{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(s_{3})]\rangle_{s_{3}}|

(5.30)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C_{\|f_{0}\|_{X}}\|J\|_{2}\frac{\lambda^{3}t^{3}}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{C\lambda|\Lambda|\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}^{3}

(5.31)

\displaystyle\,(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{6}|\Lambda|^{\frac{11}{2}}\Big(1+\frac{|\Lambda|^{\frac{1}{2}}}{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}\Big)^{3}

(5.32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\,C_{\|f_{0}\|_{X}}\|J\|_{2}\frac{1}{N^{\frac{3}{2}}}e^{C\lambda|\Lambda|\|\hat{v}\|_{Y}(1+\frac{(1+\|f_{0}\|_{X})(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})}{N})t}(1+\|\gamma_{0}\|_{X})^{6}|\Lambda|^{\frac{11}{2}}\,.

∎

Appendix A
Calculation of the fluctuation dynamics

Lemma A.1
.

We have that

(A.1)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k]a_{p}{\mathcal{T}}[k]=

\displaystyle\cosh\big(|k(p)|\big)a_{p}\,+\,\sinh\big(|k(p)|\big)\frac{k(p)}{|k(p)|}a^{\dagger}_{-p}\,.

Proof.

Observe that

(A.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k]a_{p}{\mathcal{T}}[\tau k]\\
{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k]a^{\dagger}_{-p}{\mathcal{T}}[\tau k]\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\frac{1}{2}\int dq\,k(q){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k][a_{p},a^{\dagger}_{q}a^{\dagger}_{-q}]{\mathcal{T}}[\tau k]\\
-\frac{1}{2}\int dq\,k(q){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k][a^{\dagger}_{-p},a_{q}a_{-q}]{\mathcal{T}}[\tau k]\end{pmatrix}

(A.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}0&k(p)\\
\overline{k}(p)&0\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k]a_{p}{\mathcal{T}}[\tau k]\\
{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k]a^{\dagger}_{-p}{\mathcal{T}}[\tau k]\end{pmatrix}\,,

where we used the fact that
k
is even. Employing the fact that
{\mathcal{T}}[0]={\bf 1}
and
\begin{pmatrix}0&k(p)\\
\overline{k}(p)&0\end{pmatrix}^{2}=|k(p)|^{2}{\bf 1}
, we find that

(A.4)

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k]a_{p}{\mathcal{T}}[k]\\
{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k]a^{\dagger}_{-p}{\mathcal{T}}[k]\end{pmatrix}=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(\begin{pmatrix}0&k(p)\\
\overline{k}(p)&0\end{pmatrix}\Big)\begin{pmatrix}a_{p}\\
a^{\dagger}_{-p}\end{pmatrix}

(A.5)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big[\cosh(|k(p)|){\bf 1}+\frac{\sinh(|k(p)|)}{|k(p)|}\begin{pmatrix}0&k(p)\\
\overline{k}(p)&0\end{pmatrix}\Big]\begin{pmatrix}a_{p}\\
a^{\dagger}_{-p}\end{pmatrix}\,.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)
be defined by (
2.24
) and (
2.25
). Recall from (
2.58
)–(
2.68
) the definitions of
\mathbf{w}^{(j,j)}
. By choosing

(A.6)

\displaystyle S_{t}\,=\,

\displaystyle|\Lambda|\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[E(p)\gamma_{s}(p)\,+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\Gamma^{(1)}_{s}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))(p)\Gamma^{(1)}_{s}(p)

(A.7)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\overline{\Sigma}^{(1)}_{s}*\hat{v}(p)\Sigma^{(1)}_{s}(p)\,-\,N|\Lambda|^{2}\lambda|\phi_{s}|^{4}\hat{v}(0)

(A.8)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{N|\Lambda|\delta(p)\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\phi}_{s}i\partial_{s}\phi_{s}\big)}{2}\,-\,\frac{\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\sigma}_{s}(p)i\partial_{s}\sigma_{s}(p)\big)}{2\big(1+\gamma_{s}(p)\big)}\Big]\,,

we then have that

(A.9)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)\,=\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(t)\,+\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(t)\,+\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}(t)\,+\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(t)\,,

where

(A.10)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{BEC}}(t)\,=

\displaystyle\,\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\Big[u_{t}(0)\Big(-i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}+\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(0)|\phi_{t}|^{2}\phi_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{N}(\sigma_{t}*\hat{v})(0)\overline{\phi}_{t}

(A.11)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\gamma_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(0)\phi_{t}\Big)\,+\,v_{t}(0)\Big(-\overline{i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}}+\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(0)|\phi_{t}|^{2}\overline{\phi}_{t}

(A.12)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda}{N}(\overline{\sigma}_{t}*\hat{v})(0)\phi_{t}+\frac{\lambda}{N}\big(\gamma_{t}*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(0)\overline{\phi}_{t}\Big)\Big]e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \Omega_{s}(0)}a^{\dagger}_{0}\,+\,\mathrm{h.c.}

is the BEC-Hamiltonian,

(A.13)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}(t)\,=\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(d)}}(t)\,+\,{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(cor)}}(t)

is the HFB-Hamiltonian,

(A.14)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(d)}}(t)\,=

\displaystyle\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[-\Omega_{t}(p)-\frac{\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}(p)\big)}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}

(A.15)

\displaystyle+\,\Big(E(p)+\frac{\lambda}{N}\big((\gamma_{t}+N|\Lambda||\phi_{t}|^{2}\delta)*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(p)\Big)\big(1+2\gamma_{t}(p)\big)

(A.16)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{2\lambda}{N}\operatorname{Re}\Big(\big((\overline{\sigma}_{t}+N|\Lambda|\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\delta)*\hat{v}\big)(p)\sigma_{t}(p)\Big)\Big]a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}

refers to its diagonal part,

(A.17)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{HFB}}^{\mathrm{(cor)}}(t)\,=\,

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[-\frac{i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}(p)}{2}+\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}(p)}{2(1+\gamma_{t}(p))}

(A.18)

\displaystyle+\,\Big(E(p)+\frac{\lambda}{N}\big((\gamma_{t}+N|\Lambda||\phi_{t}|^{2}\delta)*(\hat{v}+\hat{v}(0))\big)(p)\Big)\sigma_{t}(p)

(A.19)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\Big(\big((\sigma_{t}+N|\Lambda|\phi_{t}^{2}\delta)*\hat{v}\big)(p)(1+\gamma_{t}(p))

(A.20)

\displaystyle+\,\big((\overline{\sigma}_{t}+N|\Lambda|\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\delta)*\hat{v}\big)(p)\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)^{2}}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\Big)\Big]e^{2i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \Omega_{\tau}(p)}a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}\,+\,\mathrm{h.c.}

to its off-diagonal part,

(A.21)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}(t)\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{\sqrt{N}}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{3}\ \Big(\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})\big)}

(A.22)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\mathbf{w}^{(3,0)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{3})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}

(A.23)

\displaystyle+\,\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})\big)}

(A.24)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2!}\mathbf{w}^{(2,1)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{3})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}

(A.25)

\displaystyle+\,h.c.\Big)

describes the cubic processes, and

(A.26)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(t)\,=\,

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{N}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{4}\ \Big(\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{4})\big)}

(A.27)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4!}\mathbf{w}^{(4,0)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{4})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{4}}

(A.28)

\displaystyle+\,\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}-p_{4})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{4})\big)}

(A.29)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\mathbf{w}^{(3,1)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{4})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}a_{p_{4}}

(A.30)

\displaystyle+\,h.c.

(A.31)

\displaystyle+\,\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{4})\big)}

(A.32)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2!)^{2}}\mathbf{w}^{(2,2)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{4})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}a_{p_{4}}\Big)

describes quartic interactions.

Proof.

By definition, we have that

(A.33)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)=

\displaystyle-\partial_{t}S_{t}\,-\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Omega_{t}(p)a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}^{\dagger}(t)[i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}(t)

(A.34)

\displaystyle+\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}^{\dagger}(t){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}][i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]]{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}(t)

(A.35)

\displaystyle+\,{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}^{\dagger}(t){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]{\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{H}}_{N}{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}(t)\,.

In ascending order, we define
\partial_{t}S_{t}
till
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(t)
to be the normal-ordered zeroth till fourth order polynomials in
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{fluc}}(t)
.

In order to calculate
[i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}(t)]{\mathcal{T}}(t)
, we use the fact that

(A.36)

\displaystyle\big[\partial_{t}e^{-A(t)}\big]e^{A(t)}=

\displaystyle\lim_{h\to 0}\frac{1}{h}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \partial_{\tau}\Big(e^{-A(t+h)\tau}e^{A(t)\tau}\Big)

(A.37)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\ e^{-A(t)\tau}[-\partial_{t}A(t)]e^{A(t)\tau}\,,

see, e.g., the proof of Proposition 3.2 in
[
14
]
. Accordingly, we have that

(A.38)

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\ {\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[\tau k_{t}]\int\mathrm{d}p\ \big(\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)a_{p}a_{-p}\,-\,\partial_{t}k_{t}(p)a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}\big){\mathcal{T}}[\tau k_{t}]

(A.39)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \Big(\big(\cosh(\tau|k_{t}(p)|)^{2}\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)

(A.40)

\displaystyle-\,\sinh(\tau|k_{t}(p)|)^{2}\frac{\overline{k_{t}}(p)^{2}}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\partial_{t}k_{t}(p)\big)a_{p}a_{-p}\,-\,h.c.

(A.41)

\displaystyle+\,\sinh(\tau|k_{t}(p)|)\cosh(\tau|k_{t}(p)|)\frac{k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)-\partial_{t}k_{t}(p)\overline{k_{t}}(p)}{|k_{t}(p)|}

(A.42)

\displaystyle\big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,a_{p}a^{\dagger}_{p}\big)\Big)\,.

Using the trigonometric identities for
\sinh
and
\cosh
, we can simplify the expression to be

(A.43)

\displaystyle[\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\Big(\frac{\sinh(2|k_{t}(p)|)+1}{2|k_{t}(p)|}\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)

(A.44)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\sinh(2|k_{t}(p)|)-1}{2|k_{t}(p)|}\frac{\overline{k_{t}}(p)^{2}}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\partial_{t}k_{t}(p)\Big)a_{p}a_{-p}\,-\,h.c.

(A.45)

\displaystyle+\,4\frac{\cosh(2|k_{t}(p)|)-1}{2|k_{t}(p)|}\frac{i\operatorname{Im}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|}\big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,\frac{|\Lambda|}{2}\big)\Big]

(A.46)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\Big(u_{t}(p)\overline{v_{t}}(p)\frac{i\operatorname{Im}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}

(A.47)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\overline{k_{t}}(p)\operatorname{Re}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\Big)a_{p}a_{-p}\,-\,h.c.

(A.48)

\displaystyle+\,2|v_{t}(p)|^{2}\frac{i\operatorname{Im}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,\frac{|\Lambda|}{2}\big)\Big]\,.

A straightforward calculation yields

(A.49)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}u_{t}(p)=

\displaystyle v_{t}(p)\frac{\overline{k_{t}}(p)\operatorname{Re}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\,,

(A.50)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\overline{v_{t}}(p)=

\displaystyle u_{t}(p)\frac{\overline{k_{t}}(p)\operatorname{Re}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\,+\,\overline{v_{t}}(p)\frac{i\operatorname{Im}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\,.

In particular, we have that

(A.51)

\displaystyle\frac{\overline{k_{t}}(p)\operatorname{Re}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}\,+\,u_{t}(p)\overline{v_{t}}(p)\frac{i\operatorname{Im}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}

(A.52)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{v_{t}}(p)\,-\,\overline{v_{t}}(p)\partial_{t}u_{t}(p)

(A.53)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{t}(p)^{2}\partial_{t}\big(\frac{\overline{v_{t}}(p)}{u_{t}(p)}\big)\,.

Because
u_{t}(p)^{2}=1+|v_{t}(p)|^{2}
, we can invoke that

(A.54)

\displaystyle u_{t}(p)\partial_{t}u_{t}(p)\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}u_{t}(p)^{2}\,=\,\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}|v_{t}(p)|^{2}\,=\,\operatorname{Re}\big(v_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{v_{t}}(p)\big)\,,

in order to obtain that

(A.55)

\displaystyle|v_{t}(p)|^{2}\frac{i\operatorname{Im}\big(k_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{k_{t}}(p)\big)}{|k_{t}(p)|^{2}}

(A.56)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{v_{t}}(p)-u_{t}(p)\partial_{t}u_{t}(p)

(A.57)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle i\operatorname{Im}\big(v_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{v_{t}}(p)\big)

(A.58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle v_{t}(p)^{2}\partial_{t}\big(\frac{\overline{v_{t}}(p)}{v_{t}(p)}\big)\,.

Using the notation
\gamma_{t}(p)=|v_{t}(p)|^{2}
,
\sigma_{t}(p)=u_{t}(p)v_{t}(p)
, and employing (
A.53
), (
A.58
), we can hence simplify (
A.48
) as

(A.59)

\displaystyle[i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big((1+\gamma_{t}(p))\partial_{t}\big(\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\big)a_{p}a_{-p}\,-\,h.c.

(A.60)

\displaystyle+\,2\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)^{2}}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\partial_{t}\big(\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)}{\sigma_{t}(p)}\big)\big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,\frac{|\Lambda|}{2}\big)\Big)

(A.61)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\Big(\frac{\overline{i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}}(p)}{2}+\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}(p)}{2(1+\gamma_{t}(p))}\Big)a_{p}a_{-p}\,+\,\mathrm{h.c.}

(A.62)

\displaystyle-\,\frac{\operatorname{Im}\big(\sigma_{t}(p)\partial_{t}\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)\big)}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\Big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,\frac{|\Lambda|}{2}\Big)\Big]

Observing that
\operatorname{Im}(z)=\operatorname{Re}(i\overline{z})
, and using (
2.23
), (
A.62
) implies

(A.63)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}^{\dagger}(t)[i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]{\mathcal{U}}_{\mathrm{Bog}}(t)

(A.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\Big(\frac{\overline{i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}}(p)}{2}+\frac{\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\gamma_{t}(p)}{2(1+\gamma_{t}(p))}\Big)e^{-2i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}s\ \Omega_{s}(p)}a_{p}a_{-p}\,+\,\mathrm{h.c.}

(A.65)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)i\partial_{t}\sigma_{t}(p)\big)}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\Big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}\,+\,\frac{|\Lambda|}{2}\Big)\Big]

Next, using (
A.37
) again, we have that

(A.66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&[i\partial_{t}{\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]]{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]\\
&=\,-\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\int_{0}^{1}\mathrm{d}\tau\ {\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\tau\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]\big(i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}a^{\dagger}_{0}+\overline{i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}}a_{0}\big){\mathcal{W}}[\tau\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]\\
&=\,-\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\big(i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}a^{\dagger}_{0}+\overline{i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}}a_{0}\big)-\frac{N|\Lambda|^{2}}{2}\operatorname{Re}\big(\overline{\phi}_{t}i\partial_{t}\phi_{t}\big)\,,\end{split}

where we also employed that
\delta(0)=|\Lambda|
.

Finally, we calculate

(A.67)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{H}}_{N}{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]

(A.68)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N|\Lambda|^{3}\lambda|\phi_{t}|^{4}\hat{v}(0)}{2}

(A.69)

\displaystyle+\,\sqrt{N|\Lambda|^{3}}\lambda|\phi_{t}|^{2}\hat{v}(0)\big(\phi_{t}a^{\dagger}_{0}+\overline{\phi}_{t}a_{0}\big)

(A.70)

\displaystyle+\,\int\mathrm{d}p\ \big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big)a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}

(A.71)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{2}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \hat{v}(p)\big(\phi_{t}^{2}a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}+\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}a_{p}a_{-p}\big)

(A.72)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{\sqrt{N}}\int d\mathbf{p}_{2}\,\hat{v}(p_{2})\big(a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{12}}\phi_{t}\,+\,a^{\dagger}_{p_{12}}a_{p_{2}}a_{p_{1}}\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)

(A.73)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda}{2N}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{4}\ \delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})\hat{v}(p_{1}-p_{3})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}a_{p_{4}}\,.

In order to calculate

(A.74)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]{\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{H}}_{N}{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]\,,

we calculate the transformations for the scalar terms, the terms linear in
a
,
a^{\dagger}
, etc. in
{\mathcal{W}}^{\dagger}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]{\mathcal{H}}_{N}{\mathcal{W}}[\sqrt{N|\Lambda|}\phi_{t}]
. We have that

(A.75)

\displaystyle\sqrt{N|\Lambda|^{3}}\lambda|\phi_{t}|^{2}\hat{v}(0){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]\big(\phi_{t}a^{\dagger}_{0}+\overline{\phi}_{t}a_{0}\big){\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]

(A.76)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{N|\Lambda|^{3}}\lambda|\phi_{t}|^{2}\hat{v}(0)\Big(\big(\phi_{t}u_{t}(0)+\overline{\phi}_{t}v_{t}(0)\big)a^{\dagger}_{0}\,+\,\big(\overline{\phi}_{t}u_{t}(0)+\phi_{t}\overline{v_{t}}(0)\big)a_{0}\Big)\,.

For the quadratic terms, we have that

(A.77)

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ \big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}{\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]

(A.78)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{2}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \hat{v}(p){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]\big(\phi_{t}^{2}a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}+\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}a_{p}a_{-p}\big){\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]

(A.79)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big)(u_{t}(p)^{2}a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}+|v_{t}(p)|^{2}a_{p}a^{\dagger}_{p})

(A.80)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{2}\hat{v}(p)\big(u_{t}(p)\overline{v_{t}}(p)\phi_{t}^{2}+u_{t}(p)v_{t}(p)\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\big)\big(a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}+a_{p}a^{\dagger}_{p}\big)

(A.81)

\displaystyle+\,\big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big)u_{t}(p)\big(v_{t}(p)a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}+\overline{v_{t}}(p)a_{p}a_{-p}\big)

(A.82)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{2}\hat{v}(p)\Big(\big(u_{t}(p)^{2}\phi_{t}^{2}+v_{t}(p)^{2}\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\big)a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}

(A.83)

\displaystyle+\,\big(\overline{v_{t}}(p)^{2}\phi_{t}^{2}+u_{t}(p)^{2}\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\big)a_{p}a_{-p}\Big)\Big]

(A.84)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\Big(\big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big)\gamma_{t}(p)

(A.85)

\displaystyle+\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{2}\hat{v}(p)\big(\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)\phi_{t}^{2}+\sigma_{t}(p)\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\big)\Big)|\Lambda|

(A.86)

\displaystyle+\,\Big(\big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big)\big(1+2\gamma_{t}(p)\big)

(A.87)

\displaystyle+\,\lambda|\Lambda|\hat{v}(p)\big(\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)\phi_{t}^{2}+\sigma_{t}(p)\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\big)\Big)a^{\dagger}_{p}a_{p}

(A.88)

\displaystyle+\,\Big(\big(E(p)+\lambda|\Lambda|(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0))|\phi_{t}|^{2}\big)\sigma_{t}(p)

(A.89)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{\lambda|\Lambda|}{2}\hat{v}(p)\big((1+\gamma_{t}(p))\phi_{t}^{2}+\frac{\sigma_{t}(p)^{2}}{1+\gamma_{t}(p)}\overline{\phi}_{t}^{2}\big)\Big)a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}\,+\,\mathrm{h.c.}\Big]\,,

where the hermitian conjugate ’h.c.’ only refers to the terms proportional to
a^{\dagger}_{p}a^{\dagger}_{-p}
. Bogoliubov-rotating the cubic terms yields

(A.90)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{\sqrt{N}}\int d\mathbf{p}_{2}\,\hat{v}(p_{2}){\mathcal{T}}^{\dagger}[k_{t}]\big(a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{12}}\phi_{t}\,+\,a^{\dagger}_{p_{12}}a_{p_{2}}a_{p_{1}}\overline{\phi}_{t}\big){\mathcal{T}}[k_{t}]

(A.91)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{\sqrt{N}}\int d\mathbf{p}_{2}\,\frac{\hat{v}(p_{1})+\hat{v}(p_{2})}{2}\Big(\big(u_{t}(p_{1})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}+\overline{v_{t}}(p_{1})a_{-p_{1}}\big)

(A.92)

\displaystyle\big(u_{t}(p_{2})a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}+\overline{v_{t}}(p_{2})a_{-p_{2}}\big)\big(u_{t}(p_{12})a_{p_{12}}+v_{t}(p_{12})a^{\dagger}_{-p_{12}}\big)\phi_{t}\,+\,\mathrm{h.c.}\Big)\,.

After normal-ordering, the expressions linear in
a
,
a^{\dagger}
in (
A.92
) are given by

(A.93)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{\sqrt{N}}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[\Big(\hat{v}(p)\big(u_{t}(p)\overline{v_{t}}(p)v_{t}(0)\phi_{t}\,+\,u_{t}(p)v_{t}(p)u_{t}(0)\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)

(A.94)

\displaystyle+|v_{t}(p)|^{2}\big(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0)\big)\big(u_{t}(0)\phi_{t}+v_{t}(0)\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)\Big)a^{\dagger}_{0}\,+\,h.c.\Big]

(A.95)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{\sqrt{N}}\int\mathrm{d}p\ \Big[u_{t}(0)\Big(\hat{v}(p)\sigma_{t}(p)\overline{\phi}_{t}+\big(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0)\big)\gamma_{t}(p)\phi_{t}\Big)

(A.96)

\displaystyle+\,v_{t}(0)\Big(\hat{v}(p)\overline{\sigma}_{t}(p)\phi_{t}+\big(\hat{v}(p)+\hat{v}(0)\big)\gamma_{t}(p)\overline{\phi}_{t}\Big)\Big]a^{\dagger}_{0}\,+\,h.c.\,.

Similarly, the cubic terms obtained from normal-ordering (
A.92
) are given by

(A.97)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{2\sqrt{N}}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{3}\ \big(\hat{v}(p_{1})+\hat{v}(p_{2})\big)\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})

(A.98)

\displaystyle\Big(\big(u_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+v_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})\overline{v_{t}}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}

(A.99)

\displaystyle+\,\big(u_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})v_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+v_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{-p_{3}}

(A.100)

\displaystyle+\,2\big(\overline{v_{t}}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})v_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+u_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)a^{\dagger}_{-p_{3}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{-p_{1}}

(A.101)

\displaystyle+\,h.c.\Big)\,.

Here, we already exploited the symmetry w.r.t.
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
. Relabeling momenta yields

(A.102)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{2\sqrt{N}}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{3}\ \Big[\Big(\big(u_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+v_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})\overline{v_{t}}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)\big(\hat{v}(p_{1})+\hat{v}(p_{2})\big)

(A.103)

\displaystyle+2\big(v_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})\overline{v_{t}}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+u_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)\big(\hat{v}(p_{2})+\hat{v}(p_{3})\big)\Big)

(A.104)

\displaystyle\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}

(A.105)

\displaystyle+\,\big(u_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})v_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+v_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)\big(\hat{v}(p_{1})+\hat{v}(p_{2})\big)

(A.106)

\displaystyle\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}

(A.107)

\displaystyle+\,h.c.\Big)\,.

To conclude the calculation of these cubic terms, we symmetrize the coefficient of
a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}
w.r.t.
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}
, and the coefficient of
a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}
w.r.t. permutations of
(p_{1},p_{2},p_{3})
to obtain

(A.108)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda\sqrt{|\Lambda|}}{\sqrt{N}}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{3}\ \Big[\frac{1}{3!}\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}

(A.109)

\displaystyle\Big(\big(u_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})v_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+v_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)\big(\hat{v}(p_{1})+\hat{v}(p_{2})\big)

(A.110)

\displaystyle+\,\big(v_{t}(p_{1})u_{t}(p_{2})u_{t}(p_{3})\phi_{t}+u_{t}(p_{1})v_{t}(p_{2})v_{t}(p_{3})\overline{\phi}_{t}\big)\big(\hat{v}(p_{2})+\hat{v}(p_{3})\big)

(A.111)

2.68
), we obtain that

(A.205)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}(t)\,=

(A.206)

\displaystyle\frac{\lambda}{N}\int\mathrm{d}\mathbf{p}_{4}\ \Big[\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{4})\big)}

(A.207)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4!}\mathbf{w}^{(4,0)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{4})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{4}}

(A.208)

\displaystyle+\,\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}-p_{4})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{4})\big)}

(A.209)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3!}\mathbf{w}^{(3,1)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{4})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{3}}a_{p_{4}}

(A.210)

\displaystyle+\,h.c.

(A.211)

\displaystyle+\,\delta(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}-p_{4})e^{i\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{d}\tau\ \big(\Omega_{\tau}(p_{1})+\Omega_{\tau}(p_{2})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{3})-\Omega_{\tau}(p_{4})\big)}

(A.212)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2!)^{2}}\mathbf{w}^{(2,2)}_{t}(\mathbf{p}_{4})a^{\dagger}_{p_{1}}a^{\dagger}_{p_{2}}a_{p_{3}}a_{p_{4}}\,.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma A.3

(Contracted vertices)
.

{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{cub}}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathrm{quart}}
be defined as in Lemma
A.2
. Then we have

(A.213)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 6.7284pt\hbox to20.39pt{\vbox to1pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 10.19447pt\lower-0.5pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
